text-[#1e3a5f]">HPLC Purity Analysis</div><div class="font-mono text-[0.65rem] text-[#637272]">≥ 98.0% · Reversed-Phase C18 · Area Normalization</div></div><span class="shrink-0 rounded-sm px-2 py-0.5 font-mono text-[0.58rem] font-semibold tracking-[0.04em] max-[640px]:hidden bg-[#7a9e7d]/10 text-[#7a9e7d]">COMMON</span><svg width="16" height="16" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="#8a9aad" transition-all duration-350 ease-out" style="max-height:0px"><div class="border-t border-[#d6dbd7] px-5 py-5 max-[640px]:px-3.5"><div class="grid gap-6 max-[900px]:grid-cols-1 min-[901px]:grid-cols-3"><div><div class="mb-2 font-mono text-[0.58rem] font-semibold tracking-[0.1em] text-[#8099b8]">METHOD</div><p class="text-[0.8rem] leading-[1.6] text-[#454545]">Reversed-phase HPLC with UV detection at 220 nm. Purity by area normalization. Minimum acceptance: ≥98.0%.</p></div><div><div class="mb-2 font-mono text-[0.58rem] font-semibold tracking-[0.1em] text-[#8099b8]">WHAT IT MEASURES</div><p class="text-[0.8rem] leading-[1.6] text-[#454545]">Total peptide purity. Identifies co-eluting impurities, incomplete couplings, and degradation products. Chromatogram published with every COA.</p></div><div><div class="mb-2 font-mono text-[0.58rem] font-semibold tracking-[0.1em] text-[#8099b8]">LIMITATION</div><p class="text-[0.8rem] leading-[1.6] text-[#454545]">Confirms purity but not identity. A mis-synthesized peptide with the wrong sequence can read ≥99% pure. That is why ESI-MS is required as a second check.</p></div></div></div></div></div></div><div role="listitem"><div class="border border-[#d6dbd7] bg-[#f8f8f6] transition-all duration-200   -mt-px hover:border-[#9daed5]"><button class="flex w-full cursor-pointer items-center gap-4 px-5 py-4 text-left transition-colors max-[640px]:gap-3 max-[640px]:px-3.5" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="test-detail-02"><span class="flex h-8 w-8 shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded-full border border-[#d6dbd7] bg-[#f0f2ef] font-mono text-[0.68rem] font-bold text-[#1e3a5f]">02</span><div class="flex-1"><div class="font-mono text-[0.82rem] font-semibold text-[#1e3a5f]">ESI-MS Identity Confirmation</div><div class="font-mono text-[0.65rem] text-[#637272]">±0.1 Da · Electrospray Ionization · Mass Accuracy</div></div><span class="shrink-0 rounded-sm px-2 py-0.5 font-mono text-[0.58rem] font-semibold tracking-[0.04em] max-[640px]:hidden bg-[#7a9e7d]/10 text-[#7a9e7d]">COMMON</span><svg width="16" height="16" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="#8a9aad" stroke-width="2" class="shrink-0 transition-transform duration-300 " aria-hidden="true"><polyline points="6 9 12 15 18 9"></polyline></svg></button><div id="test-detail-02" class="overflow-hidden transition-all duration-350 ease-out" style="max-height:0px"><div class="border-t border-[#d6dbd7] px-5 py-5 max-[640px]:px-3.5"><div class="grid gap-6 max-[900px]:grid-cols-1 min-[901px]:grid-cols-3"><div><div class="mb-2 font-mono text-[0.58rem] font-semibold tracking-[0.1em] text-[#8099b8]">METHOD</div><p class="text-[0.8rem] leading-[1.6] text-[#454545]">Electrospray ionization mass spectrometry. Mass confirmed to ±0.1 Da of theoretical. Multiply charged ion envelope deconvoluted for intact mass.</p></div><div><div class="mb-2 font-mono text-[0.58rem] font-semibold tracking-[0.1em] text-[#8099b8]">WHAT IT MEASURES</div><p class="text-[0.8rem] leading-[1.6] text-[#454545]">Molecular identity. Confirms the synthesized peptide matches the intended sequence by mass. Spectrum published in COA.</p></div><div><div class="mb-2 font-mono text-[0.58rem] font-semibold tracking-[0.1em] text-[#8099b8]">WHY HPLC ALONE IS INSUFFICIENT</div><p class="text-[0.8rem] leading-[1.6] text-[#454545]">A peptide can show 99% chromatographic purity and still be the wrong compound. Mass spec confirms molecular identity independently.</p></div></div></div></div></div></div><div role="listitem"><div class="flex items-center gap-4 py-4"><span class="h-px flex-1 bg-[#d6dbd7]"></span><span class="font-mono text-[0.6rem] font-semibold tracking-[0.15em] text-[#8099b8]">BEYOND STANDARD PANEL</span><span class="h-px flex-1 bg-[#d6dbd7]"></span></div><div class="border border-[#d6dbd7] bg-[#f8f8f6] transition-all duration-200    hover:border-[#9daed5]"><button class="flex w-full cursor-pointer items-center gap-4 px-5 py-4 text-left transition-colors max-[640px]:gap-3 max-[640px]:px-3.5" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="test-detail-03"><span class="flex h-8 w-8 shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded-full border border-[#d6dbd7] bg-[#f0f2ef] font-mono text-[0.68rem] font-bold text-[#1e3a5f]">03</span><div class="flex-1"><div class="font-mono text-[0.82rem] font-semibold text-[#1e3a5f]">Endotoxin Screening (LAL)</div><div class="font-mono text-[0.65rem] text-[#637272]">&lt; 0.25 EU/mL · USP &lt;85&gt; · Kinetic Turbidimetric</div></div><span class="shrink-0 rounded-sm px-2 py-0.5 font-mono text-[0.58rem] font-semibold tracking-[0.04em] max-[640px]:hidden bg-[#c4a35a]/10 text-[#c4a35a]">RARE</span><svg width="16" height="16" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="#8a9aad" stroke-width="2" class="shrink-0 transition-transform duration-300 " aria-hidden="true"><polyline points="6 9 12 15 18 9"></polyline></svg></button><div id="test-detail-03" class="overflow-hidden transition-all duration-350 ease-out" style="max-height:0px"><div class="border-t border-[#d6dbd7] px-5 py-5 max-[640px]:px-3.5"><div class="grid gap-6 max-[900px]:grid-cols-1 min-[901px]:grid-cols-3"><div><div class="mb-2 font-mono text-[0.58rem] font-semibold tracking-[0.1em] text-[#8099b8]">METHOD</div><p class="text-[0.8rem] leading-[1.6] text-[#454545]">Limulus amebocyte lysate (LAL) assay per USP &lt;85&gt;. Kinetic turbidimetric method. Acceptance: &lt;0.25 EU/mL.</p></div><div><div class="mb-2 font-mono text-[0.58rem] font-semibold tracking-[0.1em] text-[#8099b8]">WHAT IT MEASURES</div><p class="text-[0.8rem] leading-[1.6] text-[#454545]">Bacterial endotoxin (lipopolysaccharide). Pyrogenic and can confound inflammatory endpoints in vivo, regardless of peptide purity.</p></div><div><div class="mb-2 font-mono text-[0.58rem] font-semibold tracking-[0.1em] text-[#8099b8]">WHY MOST VENDORS SKIP IT</div><p class="text-[0.8rem] leading-[1.6] text-[#454545]">Requires dedicated instrumentation and validated depyrogenation. Invisible to both HPLC and mass spectrometry.</p></div></div></div></div></div></div><div role="listitem"><div class="border border-[#d6dbd7] bg-[#f8f8f6] transition-all duration-200  rounded-b-sm -mt-px hover:border-[#9daed5]"><button class="flex w-full cursor-pointer items-center gap-4 px-5 py-4 text-left transition-colors max-[640px]:gap-3 max-[640px]:px-3.5" aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="test-detail-04"><span class="flex h-8 w-8 shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded-full border border-[#d6dbd7] bg-[#f0f2ef] font-mono text-[0.68rem] font-bold text-[#1e3a5f]">04</span><div class="flex-1"><div class="font-mono text-[0.82rem] font-semibold text-[#1e3a5f]">Amino Acid Analysis (AAA)</div><div class="font-mono text-[0.65rem] text-[#637272]">Peptides &gt; 15 Residues · Acid Hydrolysis · Net Peptide Content</div></div><span class="shrink-0 rounded-sm px-2 py-0.5 font-mono text-[0.58rem] font-semibold tracking-[0.04em] max-[640px]:hidden bg-[#c4a35a]/10 text-[#c4a35a]">RARE</span><svg width="16" height="16" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="#8a9aad" stroke-width="2" class="shrink-0 transition-transform duration-300 " aria-hidden="true"><polyline points="6 9 12 15 18 9"></polyline></svg></button><div id="test-detail-04" class="overflow-hidden transition-all duration-350 ease-out" style="max-height:0px"><div class="border-t border-[#d6dbd7] px-5 py-5 max-[640px]:px-3.5"><div class="grid gap-6 max-[900px]:grid-cols-1 min-[901px]:grid-cols-3"><div><div class="mb-2 font-mono text-[0.58rem] font-semibold tracking-[0.1em] text-[#8099b8]">METHOD</div><p class="text-[0.8rem] leading-[1.6] text-[#454545]">Acid hydrolysis (6N HCl, 110°C, 24 hrs) followed by chromatographic quantification of amino acid ratios. For peptides &gt;15 residues.</p></div><div><div class="mb-2 font-mono text-[0.58rem] font-semibold tracking-[0.1em] text-[#8099b8]">WHAT IT MEASURES</div><p class="text-[0.8rem] leading-[1.6] text-[#454545]">Net peptide content and amino acid composition. Confirms correct residues in expected proportions.</p></div><div><div class="mb-2 font-mono text-[0.58rem] font-semibold tracking-[0.1em] text-[#8099b8]">WHY IT MATTERS</div><p class="text-[0.8rem] leading-[1.6] text-[#454545]">Without AAA, dosing is based on gross weight including TFA counterions and moisture. AAA provides actual peptide fraction for accurate dosing.</p></div></div></div></div></div></div></div><p class="mt-8 text-[0.78rem] leading-[1.7] text-[#637272]">Tests 3–4 require dedicated instrumentation beyond standard HPLC/MS and are not part of most research peptide vendors’ standard release panel. All analytical data, including raw spectra and measured values, is published in the COA for each lot.</p></div></section><section class="relative bg-[#0f2238] py-[88px] text-[#f8f8f6]" id="coa"><div class="pointer-events-none absolute inset-0 z-[1]" style="background:repeating-linear-gradient(0deg, transparent, transparent 3px, rgba(255,255,255,0.006) 3px, rgba(255,255,255,0.006) 4px)"></div><div class="relative z-[2] mx-auto max-w-[1200px] px-6"><div class="mb-3 font-mono text-[0.6rem] tracking-[0.2em] text-white/40">COA LIBRARY</div><h2 class="mb-2.5 max-w-[720px] font-serif text-[1.75rem] font-semibold leading-tight text-[#f8f8f6] max-[640px]:text-[1.4rem]">Certificates of Analysis — published before shipment, searchable by lot number, downloadable without account creation.</h2><p class="mb-8 text-[0.9rem] text-white/75">Every lot documented before it ships — not after you request it. h-5 blur-[4px]\",\"style\":{\"background\":\"radial-gradient(ellipse at center, rgba(0,0,0,0.2) 0%, transparent 70%)\"}}]\n38:T16fc,"])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"What is BPC-157?\nBPC-157 is a synthetic pentadecapeptide composed of 15 amino acids, with the sequence Gly-Glu-Pro-Pro-Pro-Gly-Lys-Pro-Ala-Asp-Asp-Ala-Gly-Leu-Val.\nIt is a portion of a protein that occurs naturally in human gastric juice, commonly referred to as Body Protection Compound (BPC). Native BPC helps maintain gastrointestinal integrity under normal physiological conditions.\nBPC-157 is stable and soluble in water. As a synthetic peptide, BPC-157 is not produced endogenously in this exact form but is designed to replicate a biologically active fragment of the parent protein found in gastric secretions [1].\nThe peptide has been the subject of extensive preclinical research, particularly in the context of cellular proliferation, angiogenesis, and tissue repair mechanisms. BPC-157 has been studied in animal and in vitro models as a tool for investigating pathways associated with:\n\nGastrointestinal homeostasis\nVascular modulation\nCytoprotection\nInflammation modulation\n\nAll research involving BPC-157 remains within the domain of experimental studies, and it is not approved for human therapeutic use.\nWhat does BPC-157 do? The research\nInflammation and pain modulation\nBPC-157 has been extensively studied in preclinical models for its modulatory effects on inflammation, particularly in relation to tissue injury and repair processes [1].\nIn various rodent studies, administration of BPC-157 reduced markers of inflammation in models of gastrointestinal, musculoskeletal, and neural injury. By modulating inflammation, rat studies suggest that various BPC variants modulate pain, while BPC-157 predominantly reduces acute pain in incisional and formalin-induced pain [2]. In a rat model of allodynia, it also reduced pain by protecting nerve integrity from capsaicin [3].\nBPC may also modulate the nitric oxide (NO) system. It counteracts both excessive and deficient NO activity, supporting endothelial integrity and attenuating leukocyte infiltration in inflamed tissues.\nThis dual regulation may contribute to its observed ability to balance pro-inflammatory and anti-inflammatory signaling cascades [4].\nIn experimental colitis models, for example, BPC-157 administration was associated with:\n\nReduced mucosal damage\nDecreased myeloperoxidase activity (a marker of neutrophil infiltration)\nNormalization of cytokine profiles\n\nBPC-157 also promotes angiogenesis (blood vessel growth) and stabilizes vascular function at sites of injury. This angiogenic support not only facilitates tissue repair but may also limit secondary inflammation resulting from ischemia and oxidative stress [5].\nResearch in tendon and ligament injury models similarly highlights reductions in edema and inflammatory cell presence following BPC-157 exposure.\nThese findings are derived from animal studies, and while promising, they await confirmation in human clinical research to fully understand its therapeutic relevance.\nTissue healing and stem cells\nBPC-157 can facilitate regeneration across various tissue types, including:\n\nTendon\nMuscle\nLigament\nBone\nNerve\n\nA key feature identified in these models is BPC-157’s capacity to modulate cellular and immune environments in ways that promote structural integrity and restoration of injured sites.\nIn rat models of tendon fibroblasts, BPC-157 significantly upregulated growth hormone receptor expression, and enhanced the responsiveness of these cells to endogenous growth hormone [6].\nThis interaction promotes cell proliferation and tissue regeneration, increasing expression of proliferation markers:\n\nProliferating cell nuclear antigen (PCNA)\nJAK2 signaling pathway\n\nThese results suggest a supportive role in tendon repair processes at the molecular level, enhancing the body's natural regenerative mechanisms.\nBPC-157 may influence stem cell activity indirectly by optimizing the local microenvironment of injured tissues.\nWhile direct stimulation of stem cell differentiation by BPC-157 has not been definitively proven, its actions on surrounding tissues, blood vessels, and extracellular matrix components are believed to indirectly enhance stem cell-mediated repair processes.\nGut health\nBPC-157 has been extensively studied in preclinical models for its protective and regenerative effects on the gastrointestinal (GI) tract [7].\nResearch in rat models of ileoileal anastomosis healing, for instance, shows that BPC-157:\n\nModulates local immune responses\nEnhances granulation tissue formation\nIncreases collagen and reticulin deposition\nPromotes re-epithelialization\nSupports the regeneration of muscular tissue strands at anastomotic sites.\n\nBPC-157 reduced adhesion formation and necrosis, while accelerating the resolution of edema and inflammatory infiltrates in treated animals compared to controls.\nAdditional experimental models of intestinal injury, including those involving perforations, fistulas, or induced colitis, report that BPC-157 administration supported gut integrity by:\n\nPromoting angiogenesis\nMitigating tissue necrosis\nStabilizing microvascular structures\n\nThe peptide’s influence on nitric oxide pathways and its modulation of endothelial function are proposed mechanisms underlying these benefits.\nWhile clinical trials in humans remain limited, the consistent findings across animal models offer a compelling basis for future investigation into its application in gut health contexts.\nBPC-157 in bees\nBees are crucial pollinators whose populations are in significant declines. Research suggests that BPC-157 may help to improve bee health and survival. In bees, BPC-157 improves colony strength and enhances certain aspects of immune responses [8]. Supplementation of bee diets with the peptide reduced the infection load of the Nosema ceranae, a one-celled fungal parasite. BPC-157 also attenuates gut damage from N. ceranae infections [8]. Overall, BPC-157 may be beneficial in beekeeping."])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"39:T1c3f,"])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"\u003ch2\u003eWhat is BPC-157?\u003c/h2\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eBPC-157 is a synthetic pentadecapeptide composed of 15 amino acids, with the sequence Gly-Glu-Pro-Pro-Pro-Gly-Lys-Pro-Ala-Asp-Asp-Ala-Gly-Leu-Val.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eIt is a portion of a protein that occurs naturally in human gastric juice, commonly referred to as Body Protection Compound (BPC). Though structurally simpler than the full-length thymosin beta-4, TB500 peptide retains potent biological activity [5]. The number 500 in TB500 is added as a commercial name, without biological or scientific significance.\n\u0026nbsp;\nKey biochemical features include:\n \n\nActin-binding domain [6]: Essential for cytoskeletal regulation, enabling cell migration and tissue remodelling.\nEssential amino acid residues [6]: Support actin polymerization and cellular mobility.\nNo post-transitional modifications [6]: As a synthetic peptide, TB500 lacks glycosylation or phosphorylation, ensuring structural stability.\n\n\u0026nbsp;\nWhat does TB500 do?\nTB500 exerts multi-system effects, supporting wound healing, reducing inflammation, promoting cell regeneration, and enhancing immune defenses [1].\nTissue repair and regeneration\nTB500 accelerates tissue repair by binding actin, a key structural protein in cells. This interaction stimulates stem cell recruitment and differentiation at injury sites, migration of skin cells to close wounds faster, and formation of new blood vessels (angiogenesis) to improve oxygen and nutrient delivery [1].\nIt also enhances collagen alignment and increases laminin-5, both essential for strong and well-structured tissues [7]. Simultaneously, it reduces the number of scar-forming cells, minimizing fibrotic tissue formation [8].\nAnimal studies have confirmed TB500 peptide’s ability to reduce tissue damage, speed up recovery, and promote healing even in challenging conditions [9]. Human trials suggest that topical formulations are safe and effective in wound repair. Emerging data also support the potential role of TB500 in neurological and cardiac tissue regeneration, aiding recovery after events like stroke or heart attack [9].\nAnti-inflammatory and immunomodulatory effects\nFollowing tissue injury, high levels of inflammation can damage tissues and lead to permanent scarring. TB500 peptide mitigates this response, lowering the levels of inflammatory cells and the chemical signals they release [1]. This has downstream impacts of reducing tissue swelling, protecting healthy tissue, and creating an environment supportive of proper healing with less scar tissue formation [1].\n\u0026nbsp;\nA key anti-inflammatory mechanism involves the NF-kB signalling pathway, which controls the expression of many pro-inflammatory genes. TB500 inhibits NF-kB activation, prevents p65 subunit phosphorylation, and blocks nuclear translocation of NF-kB [10]. These actions have been demonstrated in corneal, cardiac, and liver tissues. The NF-kB inhibition contributes to reduced inflammation and improved healing responses in these tissues [10].\n\u0026nbsp;\nTB500 peptide modulates the toll-like receptor-4 (TLR-4) pathway, which is central to innate immune responses [11]. Through upregulation of microRNA-146a, TB500 can suppress this pathway, promoting anti-inflammatory effects [11]. For this reason, TB500 may indirectly support the repair of gut barriers by improving the proliferation and migration of cells, and supporting tissue healing processes.\nFurthermore, a peptide fragment within TB500, Ac-SDKP, has been shown to reduce fibrosis (e.g., heart scarring after myocardial infarction), likely through similar anti-inflammatory and anti-proliferative mechanisms [6].\nAntibacterial and antiviral effects\nTB500 strengthens antimicrobial defenses by increasing the expression of antimicrobial peptides (AMPs) such as keratin 6A, CAMP, beta-defensins (BD2, BD3), and S100A8 [12]. These peptides help prevent bacterial adherence and enhance immune clearance of pathogens [12].\nTB500 also boosts TLR4 expression, enhancing the recognition of bacterial invaders like LPS-producing pathogens [12]. When combined with antibiotics, TB500 enhances the activity of 12-LOX and 15-LOX enzymes, which promote resolution of inflammation and tissue restoration [12].\nThis synergy highlights TB500’s potential as an adjunct to antimicrobial therapies—supporting not only microbial defense but also repair of infected tissues."])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"3b:T1654,"])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"\u003ch2\u003eWhat is TB500 (Thymosin Beta 4)?\u003c/h2\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eTB500 peptide is a shorter, bioactive fragment of thymosin beta-4, designed to focus on thymosin beta-4’s most therapeutically relevant region, the 7-amino acid sequence LKKTETQ responsible for actin binding and tissue regeneration \u003ca href=\"https://paperpile.com/c/x8kUlV/4U7V\"\u003e[1]\u003c/a\u003e. For this reason, TB500 may indirectly support the repair of gut barriers by improving the proliferation and migration of cells, and supporting tissue healing processes.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eFurthermore, a peptide fragment within TB500, Ac-SDKP, has been shown to reduce fibrosis (e.g., heart scarring after myocardial infarction), likely through similar anti-inflammatory and anti-proliferative mechanisms \u003ca href=\"https://paperpile.com/c/x8kUlV/ZJYF\"\u003e[6]\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch3\u003eAntibacterial and antiviral effects\u003c/h3\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eTB500 strengthens antimicrobial defenses by increasing the expression of antimicrobial peptides (AMPs) such as keratin 6A, CAMP, beta-defensins (BD2, BD3), and S100A8 \u003ca href=\"https://paperpile.com/c/x8kUlV/Jmvk\"\u003e[12]\u003c/a\u003e. These peptides help prevent bacterial adherence and enhance immune clearance of pathogens \u003ca href=\"https://paperpile.com/c/x8kUlV/Jmvk\"\u003e[12]\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eTB500 also boosts TLR4 expression, enhancing the recognition of bacterial invaders like LPS-producing pathogens \u003ca href=\"https://paperpile.com/c/x8kUlV/Jmvk\"\u003e[12]\u003c/a\u003e. When combined with antibiotics, TB500 enhances the activity of 12-LOX and 15-LOX enzymes, which promote resolution of inflammation and tissue restoration \u003ca href=\"https://paperpile.com/c/x8kUlV/Jmvk\"\u003e[12]\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eThis synergy highlights TB500’s potential as an adjunct to antimicrobial therapies—supporting not only microbial defense but also repair of infected tissues.\u003c/p\u003e\n"])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"3c:T109f,"])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"What is GHK-Cu?\nNative GHK-Cu peptide is released from the extracellular matrix, cleaved from its parent protein, SPARC (secreted protein, acidic, and rich in cysteine), during extracellular matrix remodelling. Studies find it has vital roles in copper transport, tissue repair, inflammation modulation, and gene regulation [1].\nA tripeptide, GHK-Cu is composed of glycine (Gly), histidine (His), and lysine (Lys) [1]. This amino acid complex binds tightly to copper, forming a stable coordination complex [2]. This binding capacity is what gives GHK-Cu its unique biological signalling and regenerative properties [2]. After performing its functions, GHK-Cu is degraded by serum peptidases. Studies show that circulating levels decrease with age, averaging 200 ng/mL at age twenty and declining to 80 ng/mL by age sixty, potentially impacting regenerative capacity [1].\nCopper(II) is a redox-active metal required as a cofactor for many enzymes, including cytochrome C oxidase, lysyl oxidase, and superoxide dismutase [2]. GHK binds copper in a bioavailable, non-toxic form, supports shuttling it into cells, and maintains intracellular copper homeostasis [2].\nGHK-Cu shows abilities to up- or downregulate over 4,000 genes. These pathways are many, and include those involved in tissue regeneration and repair, anti-inflammatory signalling, antioxidant defenses, and cell growth and differentiation [2].\nWhile scientists are still elucidating some of GHK-Cu peptide’s mechanisms, current research suggests that they include augmenting transcription factors, epigenetic modification (particularly histone and chromatin) and oxidative stress signalling [2].\nWhat does GHK-Cu do? The research\nResearch is still emerging on the wide applications of GHK-Cu peptide; however, our current understanding of its actions point towards applications particularly in skin and hair, and regenerative processes.\nGHK-Cu and skin\nResearch suggests that GHK-Cu peptide may have roles in reprogramming older tissues to act more youthful [2]. When skin gets injured, GHK-Cu is naturally released, acting as an emergency signal to activate skin healing processes [3].\nA study using a test tube wound model found that GHK-Cu increases the production of collagen (which gives skin structure), elastin (which keeps skin elastic), and decorin and glycosaminoglycans (key molecules that hydrate and organize skin tissue) [4].\nGHK-Cu also supports the balance of enzymes that break down skin proteins, MMPs, and their inhibitors, TIMPs [5],[6]. This prevents the buildup of damaged proteins, and overactive breakdown, which can lead to thinning and sagging skin [2].\nOne cell-based study found that skin cells exposed to GHK-Cu in combination with red LED light had 12.5x greater cell survival, 230% increase in fibroblast growth factor, and 70% higher collagen production [7].\nAt the stem cell level, GHK-Cu improved the health and shape of basal cells in the epidermis, increasing markers of stemness, which may help skin regenerate better with age [8].\nGHK-Cu and hair\nGHK-Cu peptides show benefits for supporting hair growth, by [9]:\n\nImproving blood flow to hair follicles\nPreventing hair shedding and premature hair loss\nStimulating the growth of new hair follicles\n\nStudies find that GHK-Cu can stimulate fibroblasts, a particular type of skin cell, to produce VEGF (vascular endothelial growth factor), which helps support the growth of new blood vessels surrounding the hair follicle [9]. More blood vessels allow for greater nutrient and oxygen delivery to the area, thus supporting stronger, faster growing hair [9].\nIt also reduces the production of TGF-beta, a chemical that signals to hair follicles to stop growing and enter the shedding phase of hair growth [9]. With less TGF-beta, hair remains in the growth phase for longer.\nBy encouraging dermal papilla cells to multiply and protecting them from apoptosis, GHK-Cu supports the development and growth of new hair, by keeping follicles healthy [9].\nOther regenerative processes\nWhile more research is needed, our understanding of GHK-Cu points towards potential applications of this peptide in wound healing and repair processes, promotion of antioxidant defenses, angiogenesis, gene modulation, and more [2]."])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"3d:T160c,"])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"\u003ch2\u003eWhat is GHK-Cu?\u003c/h2\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eNative GHK-Cu peptide is released from the extracellular matrix, cleaved from its parent protein, SPARC (secreted protein, acidic, and rich in cysteine), during extracellular matrix remodelling.
